import cors from 'cors'
import express, { type ErrorRequestHandler } from 'express'
import { pinoHttp } from 'pino-http'
import { config } from './config.js'
import { getDatabaseStatus } from './db/status.js'

export const app = express()

app.disable('x-powered-by')
app.use(cors({ origin: config.WEB_ORIGIN, methods: ['GET', 'POST', 'PUT', 'PATCH', 'DELETE'], credentials: false }))
app.use(pinoHttp({ level: config.NODE_ENV === 'production' ? 'info' : 'debug' }))
app.use(express.json({ limit: '1mb' }))

app.get('/api/health', (_request, response) => {
  response.json({
    status: 'ok',
    service: 'careeros-api',
    mode: 'foundation-only',
    database: getDatabaseStatus(),
  })
})

app.use((_request, response) => {
  response.status(404).json({ error: 'Not found' })
})

const errorHandler: ErrorRequestHandler = (error: unknown, _request, response, _next) => {
  response.req.log.error({ err: error }, 'Unhandled API request error')
  response.status(500).json({ error: 'An unexpected server error occurred.' })
}

app.use(errorHandler)

import { dirname, resolve } from 'node:path'
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url'
import dotenv from 'dotenv'
import { z } from 'zod'

dotenv.config({ path: resolve(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '../.env') })

function isPostgreSQLUrl(value: string) {
  try {
    return ['postgres:', 'postgresql:'].includes(new URL(value).protocol)
  } catch {
    return false
  }
}

const environmentSchema = z.object({
  PORT: z.coerce.number().int().positive().default(3001),
  WEB_ORIGIN: z.string().url().default('http://localhost:5173'),
  NODE_ENV: z.enum(['development', 'test', 'production']).default('development'),
  DATABASE_URL: z.string().url().refine(isPostgreSQLUrl, 'DATABASE_URL must use PostgreSQL').optional(),
})

const result = environmentSchema.safeParse(process.env)
if (!result.success) {
  throw new Error(`Invalid API environment configuration: ${result.error.issues.map((issue) => `${issue.path.join('.')}: ${issue.message}`).join('; ')}`)
}

export const config = result.data

FILE: api/src/db/check-connection.ts
SIZE: 1339 bytes
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
import { disconnectPrismaClient, getPrismaClient } from './client.js'

async function checkConnection() {
  const prisma = getPrismaClient()
  try {
    await prisma.$connect()
    const tables = await prisma.$queryRaw<Array<{ table_name: string }>>`
      SELECT table_name
      FROM information_schema.tables
      WHERE table_schema = 'public'
        AND table_type = 'BASE TABLE'
        AND table_name <> '_prisma_migrations'
      ORDER BY table_name
    `
    console.info('PostgreSQL connection succeeded.')
    console.info(tables.length
      ? `Existing public tables: ${tables.map(({ table_name }) => table_name).join(', ')}`
      : 'No non-Prisma tables found in the public schema.')
  } finally {
    await disconnectPrismaClient()
  }
}

checkConnection().catch((error: unknown) => {
  const code = error instanceof Error && 'code' in error && typeof error.code === 'string' ? error.code : 'unknown'
  const databaseCode = error instanceof Error && 'meta' in error && error.meta && typeof error.meta === 'object' && 'code' in error.meta && typeof error.meta.code === 'string'
    ? error.meta.code
    : undefined
  console.error(`PostgreSQL connection check failed (${code}${databaseCode ? `/${databaseCode}` : ''}). Verify api/.env without sharing its secret.`)
  process.exitCode = 1
})

FILE: api/src/db/client.ts
SIZE: 1751 bytes
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
import fs from 'node:fs'
import { dirname, resolve } from 'node:path'
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url'
import { PrismaPg } from '@prisma/adapter-pg'
import { PrismaClient } from '@prisma/client'
import pg from 'pg'
import { config } from '../config.js'

let prisma: PrismaClient | undefined
let pool: pg.Pool | undefined

export function getPrismaClient() {
  if (!config.DATABASE_URL) throw new Error('DATABASE_URL must be configured before database access.')
  if (prisma) return prisma

  const certPath = resolve(dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '../../prod-ca-2021.crt')
  const ca = fs.existsSync(certPath) ? fs.readFileSync(certPath, 'utf8') : undefined

  const parsed = new URL(config.DATABASE_URL)
  const isSupabase = parsed.hostname.includes('supabase.co') || parsed.hostname.includes('supabase.com')

  if (ca && isSupabase) {
    pool = new pg.Pool({
      host: parsed.hostname,
      port: Number(parsed.port) || 5432,
      user: decodeURIComponent(parsed.username),
      password: decodeURIComponent(parsed.password),
      database: parsed.pathname.replace(/^\//, '') || 'postgres',
      ssl: {
        rejectUnauthorized: true,
        ca,
        servername: parsed.hostname,
      },
    })
    prisma = new PrismaClient({
      adapter: new PrismaPg(pool),
    })
  } else {
    pool = new pg.Pool({
      connectionString: config.DATABASE_URL,
    })
    prisma = new PrismaClient({
      adapter: new PrismaPg(pool),
    })
  }

  return prisma
}

export async function disconnectPrismaClient() {
  if (!prisma) return
  const currentPrisma = prisma
  const currentPool = pool
  prisma = undefined
  pool = undefined
  await currentPrisma.$disconnect()
  if (currentPool) {
    await currentPool.end()
  }
}

export type DatabaseStatus = 'not-configured' | 'configured' | 'connected' | 'unavailable'

let databaseStatus: DatabaseStatus = 'not-configured'

export function setDatabaseStatus(status: DatabaseStatus) {
  databaseStatus = status
}

export function getDatabaseStatus() {
  return databaseStatus
}

FILE: api/src/server.ts
SIZE: 1756 bytes
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
import { app } from './app.js'
import { config } from './config.js'
import { disconnectPrismaClient, getPrismaClient } from './db/client.js'
import { setDatabaseStatus } from './db/status.js'

async function startServer() {
  if (config.DATABASE_URL) {
    setDatabaseStatus('configured')
    try {
      await getPrismaClient().$queryRaw`SELECT 1`
      setDatabaseStatus('connected')
    } catch (error: unknown) {
      const code = error instanceof Error && 'code' in error && typeof error.code === 'string' ? error.code : 'unknown'
      setDatabaseStatus('unavailable')
      console.error(`CareerOS PostgreSQL connection is unavailable (${code}); the demo frontend and health endpoint remain usable.`)
      await disconnectPrismaClient()
    }
  }

  const server = app.listen(config.PORT, () => {
    console.info(`CareerOS API listening on http://localhost:${config.PORT}`)
  })

  server.on('error', (error: NodeJS.ErrnoException) => {
    console.error('CareerOS API failed to start.', error.code ?? 'unknown server error')
    process.exitCode = 1
  })

  const shutdown = () => {
    server.close((error) => {
      if (error) {
        console.error('CareerOS API failed to close cleanly.')
        process.exitCode = 1
      }
      void disconnectPrismaClient().catch(() => {
        console.error('CareerOS could not close the PostgreSQL connection cleanly.')
        process.exitCode = 1
      })
    })
    console.info('CareerOS API is shutting down.')
  }

  process.once('SIGINT', shutdown)
  process.once('SIGTERM', shutdown)
}

startServer().catch(() => {
  console.error('CareerOS API could not start. Verify its configuration without sharing secrets.')
  process.exitCode = 1
})

# Authentication & Authorization Design

Status: design-only; no authentication implementation has been added. `User.supabaseAuthId` and the `20261025000000_add_supabase_auth_id` migration already exist, and the migration has been applied successfully. This document reflects the current repo state and is intended to be approved before any protected CRUD or authentication code is implemented.

## 1. Current-state findings from the repository

The repo is currently in a demo-data mode and is not yet using a user-scoped API or auth-protected routes.

- Frontend: `src/App.tsx` wraps the app in `BrowserRouter` and `DemoCareerDataProvider`, and the provider persists local demo records to browser `localStorage` under the `careeros-demo-v1` key. This is a browser-local demo workflow, not a user identity system.
- Backend: `api/src/app.ts` exposes only `GET /api/health` and no auth middleware, route guards, or user-scoped CRUD. `api/src/config.ts` validates only `PORT`, `WEB_ORIGIN`, `NODE_ENV`, and `DATABASE_URL`.
- Database: `prisma/schema.prisma` includes a `User` model with `id` (CUID), `name`, `email`, and a nullable unique `supabaseAuthId` field of PostgreSQL UUID type. The `prisma/migrations/20261025000000_add_supabase_auth_id/migration.sql` migration adds that field and its unique index; it has already been applied successfully. Every other domain model (`Company`, `Contact`, `JobApplication`, `Task`, `Event`, `Activity`) includes a required `userId` and composite unique constraints to enforce same-user ownership.
- Supabase configuration: there is no current Supabase browser client, auth config, or server-side auth helper in the repo. The existing app does not yet read `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, or server-side verification configuration from environment config. A service-role key is not required solely to verify user tokens.
- Database connectivity: `DATABASE_URL` is validated, but the project currently has no authenticated API flow and no app-level user provisioning pipeline.

This design assumes all of the above stays true until explicit approval is given to implement the auth layer.

## 2. Goals and design principles

1. Supabase Auth is the source of truth for credentials, email confirmation, session management, password reset, and token lifecycle.
2. The internal Prisma `User` table is the application identity mapping layer. It must not be trusted unless it is resolved from a verified Supabase identity.
3. Ownership is enforced server-side from the authenticated internal user ID, never from a client-supplied `userId`, `ownerId`, `companyId`, or similar request field.
4. Protected endpoints validate tokens on every request and reject invalid, expired, unconfirmed, or mismatched identities with a consistent 401/403/404 response model.
5. The app must remain intentionally minimal and production-conscious: use the vendor-supported auth session behaviors and avoid building a second custom auth system.

## 3. Authentication architecture

### A. Responsibilities

#### Supabase Auth
Supabase Auth owns:
- email/password sign-up and sign-in
- email confirmation flow
- password reset and recovery
- session creation and refresh
- JWT issuance and expiry rules
- account disablement and recovery status
- browser-safe session storage and session restoration

It does not own the application’s business-level ownership rules; those remain in the Express + Prisma layer.

#### React frontend + Supabase browser client
The frontend is responsible for:
- creating the browser client with `SUPABASE_URL` + `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`
- rendering sign-up, sign-in, confirming email, password reset, and recovery states
- using the Supabase session to obtain the current access token
- storing the session via the SDK’s supported storage strategy (not manually in `localStorage`)
- attaching `Authorization: Bearer <token>` to protected API requests
- redirecting unauthenticated users away from protected routes
- restoring auth state after refresh via the SDK session lifecycle

The frontend must never store a service-role key or any auth secret in browser code.

#### Express API
The API is responsible for:
- accepting bearer tokens from the request
- verifying the token server-side using a trusted Supabase validation mechanism
- resolving the verified Supabase user UUID to the internal application `User.id` using `User.supabaseAuthId`
- attaching the authenticated internal user to a trusted request context (for example `req.auth.userId` or `req.userId`)
- applying ownership checks in every service/repository operation before reading, updating, or deleting records
- returning stable, explicit auth and authorization errors

The API must not trust client-supplied identity claims. It must always derive identity from a cryptographically verified token.

#### Prisma ORM and PostgreSQL database
Prisma and PostgreSQL enforce the application’s data model and relationships. They provide:
- a unique `User.supabaseAuthId` identity mapping
- required `userId` ownership on every domain record
- composite unique/relationship constraints that prevent cross-user associations
- server-side authorization checks at the repository/service layer

For initial scope, the app should keep auth enforcement in the Express layer and service logic, not in a public database access model.

### B. Selected verification method

Recommended choice for this repo: validate the bearer token through Supabase’s own verification endpoint/client (`supabase.auth.getUser(token)`) instead of manually decoding a JWT.

Why this is a good fit here:
- it is the supported, vendor-backed verification path for Supabase-issued tokens
- it handles token expiry and malformed token states consistently
- it avoids trusting raw JWT claims without signature validation
- it matches the requested architecture and the current project is not yet using a custom JWT stack

Implementation pattern:
- the frontend obtains a valid Supabase session and sends the access token in `Authorization: Bearer <token>`
- the API creates a server-side Supabase client using `SUPABASE_URL` and the least-privileged supported key (the publishable key, or legacy anon key, where applicable), with the request access token passed to `getUser(token)`
- the server calls `supabase.auth.getUser(token)` on the request token
- if Supabase rejects the token as invalid or expired, respond with `401 Unauthorized`; if verification is temporarily unavailable, fail closed with a service error such as `503 Service Unavailable` and do not continue to database access
- if the token is valid, read `user.id` from the verified Supabase identity and resolve the internal application `User` row using `supabaseAuthId`

Important constraints:
- use the least-privileged supported server configuration for token verification; do not require or use a service-role key solely for `getUser(token)`
- a privileged credential may be introduced only for a separately identified operation that specifically requires elevated Supabase Auth permissions; that is outside this design's token-verification flow
- never decode a JWT blob directly and trust `sub`, `email`, or any other claim without server-side verification
- never accept identity from a request body or query string
- never add token validation logic that bypasses Supabase verification for convenience
- fail closed on upstream verification failures: rejected credentials are `401`, while temporary verification-service failures are `503`; neither case may proceed as authenticated

### C. Intended request flow

1. The frontend signs the user up or logs in through Supabase Auth.
2. Supabase manages email confirmation, password reset, and the session lifecycle.
3. The frontend receives the session and extracts the current access token.
4. The frontend calls protected endpoints with `Authorization: Bearer <access-token>`.
5. Express verifies the token using a supported Supabase mechanism.
6. Express checks the verified identity's email-confirmation status server-side, then resolves the internal `User.id` using `User.supabaseAuthId`; only the designated first-time provisioning flow may create a missing mapping.
7. Express stores the trusted internal user ID on the request context.
8. Repositories and service functions scope all database operations to `req.auth.userId`.

## 4. Signup and internal user provisioning

### A. Safe flow

The flow should be:
1. User signs up with email/password in Supabase Auth.
2. Supabase creates the auth user and the email confirmation workflow begins.
3. The app’s UI checks the Supabase session and displays the confirmation state, but this is only a UX aid and does not authorize API access.
4. The server verifies the token and checks the verified Supabase identity's email-confirmation status before any provisioning or protected data access.
5. A dedicated first-time onboarding/provisioning flow may create an internal record for a confirmed identity if no `supabaseAuthId` mapping exists. Ordinary protected API requests must not auto-provision missing mappings.
6. The server ensures the internal `User.email` field is set only from the verified identity, using the same trusted identity source.

### B. Required constraints

- `User.supabaseAuthId` is the authoritative identity mapping and must be unique.
- Keep the existing internal `User.id` and all user-owned relations unchanged.
- Never auto-link a user to an existing internal account because the email strings match.
- Never create or update an internal user from an unverified request body or an untrusted client value.
- A missing mapping on an ordinary protected API request is rejected (for example, `403 ACCOUNT_NOT_PROVISIONED`); it must not trigger implicit account creation. The dedicated onboarding flow may create a mapping only after token verification and server-side email-confirmation checks.
- Never match or link an existing internal account by email alone. If the confirmed identity's email is already used by a different internal user, fail provisioning with a generic `409 ACCOUNT_MAPPING_CONFLICT`; do not disclose the other account or alter either mapping.
- Fail closed if a verified identity has no usable confirmed email, or if provisioning encounters a database error. Do not grant access on partial or failed provisioning.
- Handle duplicate provisioning attempts and concurrent requests safely using the unique `supabaseAuthId` constraint and conflict-safe logic.

### C. Concurrency and duplicate handling

Recommended approach:
- in the dedicated onboarding flow, use a transaction around lookup/create of the internal user
- attempt to find the user by `supabaseAuthId`
- if it does not exist and the identity is confirmed, create it with a unique guard on `supabaseAuthId`; if the email uniqueness constraint conflicts, return the generic conflict response and do not link by email
- if concurrent requests race, catch the `supabaseAuthId` unique-constraint error and re-read by that same verified ID; proceed only if the resulting mapping belongs to that ID, otherwise fail closed
- if a regular protected request finds no mapping, reject it and require the dedicated onboarding or account-resolution flow; do not recreate a deleted mapping automatically
- treat the operation as idempotent: same verified identity must resolve to the same internal `User.id`

This prevents duplicate internal user rows and avoids a “create-on-every-request” provisioning bug.

### D. Email confirmation impact

Supabase email confirmation governs whether the user is allowed to operate in the application.

Recommended behavior:
- sign-up is accepted and the user is created in Supabase Auth immediately
- email confirmation is required before the app unlocks protected features
- when a user is unconfirmed, the frontend redirects to a “check your email”/“confirm to continue” state
- Express must enforce confirmation on every protected API request using the confirmation status returned with the server-verified Supabase user (for example, a non-null `email_confirmed_at`/`confirmed_at` value). A missing or unconfirmed status is rejected with `403 EMAIL_NOT_CONFIRMED` before provisioning or accessing application data; never rely on a frontend check or request-supplied claim for this decision.
- once email is confirmed, the app resumes normal session management and protected route access

### E. Email change and duplicate-user scenarios

- `User.email` remains a business record, but it is not the identity anchor for access.
- If a Supabase user changes their email, the server should update the internal `User.email` only after validating the trusted identity and checking collisions.
- If the new email already belongs to another internal user, do not silently merge identities; fail the update with a generic conflict and require a manual account-resolution step.
- If the internal mapping is missing on a protected request, reject it rather than guessing or creating a fallback identity. A mapping may only be created through the dedicated first-time provisioning flow described above; do not recreate a deleted mapping automatically.

No schema changes or migrations are required for this phase. The current `supabaseAuthId` field already supports the required mapping pattern.

## 5. Session lifecycle

### Signup and email confirmation
- Sign-up happens via Supabase Auth.
- The app should surface an email-confirmation message and block access to protected pages until confirmation succeeds.
- The app should not assume an unconfirmed user is ready to access user-scoped data.

### Login
- Login uses email/password through Supabase.
- The browser client stores the session using the SDK’s supported session storage strategy.
- The app reloads its auth state from the stored session after page refresh.

### Restoring the session after page refresh
- Use the Supabase browser client’s hydration/session restore mechanism.
- Avoid manually writing auth tokens to `localStorage` or any custom storage outside the SDK.
- Restore app state only after the SDK confirms the user session is valid.

### Access-token refresh
- Supabase handles token refresh automatically according to the provider’s configured expiration behavior.
- The frontend should react to refresh errors by signing the user out and redirecting them to login while preserving a clear error message.
- The API should treat expired or otherwise invalid tokens as `401 Unauthorized` and not attempt to infer permission from stale state.

### Logout
- Use Supabase sign-out and clear all app-local auth state.
- Clear any in-memory auth state, query caches, and route guards that depend on auth.
- Do not leave a stale session token in any app-level cache or browser storage outside the SDK’s supported flow.

### Password reset and recovery
- Use Supabase password reset email flow for “forgot password”.
- Use the recovery callback with a safe redirect URL to a reset page.
- The app should handle invalid or expired reset links by showing a retry path and resetting the UI state without exposing sensitive details.

### Password update
- After user authentication, allow the user to change their password through the Supabase Auth API.
- Require reauthentication when the system policy demands it; do not create a separate custom password system.

### Redirect URLs and environments
- Use explicit redirect URLs for local development and production, e.g. local front-end callback pages and production app URLs.
- Keep redirect URLs in environment config and never embed auth metadata in query strings beyond the provider-generated callback parameters.
- Ensure app URLs are known and allowed by Supabase Auth settings.

## 6. Authorization and data ownership

### Required rules

- Every protected API endpoint must authenticate the caller before accessing any data.
- No request can determine ownership from `userId`, `ownerId`, `companyId`, or similar body/query fields.
- The server resolves the internal user ID only from the verified token identity.
- All reads, writes, updates, and deletes are scoped to that authenticated user.
- Creates assign `userId` server-side and never accept it from the client.
- Updates and deletes must verify both record existence and ownership before modifying data.
- Related records must also be checked against the same user.

### Scope rules for all domain models

The following rules apply across `Company`, `Contact`, `JobApplication`, `Task`, `Event`, and `Activity`:
- read queries must include `userId = req.auth.userId`
- updates/deletes must use a same-user and same-record constraint
- nested relations must be checked as part of the service logic
- search, filters, pagination, exports, calendar events, tasks, and activity streams must all be narrowed to the authenticated user

### Protected route responses

Use these responses consistently:
- `401 Unauthorized`: missing, malformed, invalid, expired, or unverifiable bearer token.
- `403 Forbidden`: the token is valid, but the identity is unconfirmed, has no provisioned internal mapping, or is not permitted to perform the requested action.
- `404 Not Found`: an owner-scoped lookup finds no record. Use the same response for a nonexistent record and a record owned by someone else; do not reveal whether another user's record exists.

Use `403` for identity or action-level denial, and `404` for record-level misses after applying the owner scope. Do not return `403` for a cross-owner record lookup, because that would disclose that the record exists.

### Input validation and mass-assignment defense

Every request should validate:
- params
- query strings
- body schema
- nested record references
- user-controlled foreign keys

The server must explicitly reject or ignore fields like:
- `userId`
- `supabaseAuthId`
- `ownerId`
- any auth-related metadata

Mass assignment must be prevented by using a strict DTO parser and explicit property mapping into the database layer.

## 7. Prisma, PostgreSQL, and RLS

### Current Prisma design
The schema already has appropriate same-user ownership patterns:
- `User` owns each domain table through `userId`
- composite unique constraints such as `@@unique([id, userId])` prevent cross-user associations
- relation fields include `onDelete: Cascade` for the user-owned records and `Restrict` for dependent record links

This is a strong app-layer foundation and aligns with the intended authorization model.

### Recommended using Prisma + app-layer auth
For the initial release, the safest and most maintainable design is:
- Prisma handles DB mapping and transactions
- Express handles authentication and authorization context
- the request layer derives `userId` from the verified identity and scopes every DB query to that user

This is a good fit for the current architecture and keeps the auth decision inside a single, testable security boundary.

### Row-level security (RLS)
RLS is optional for defense in depth but is not required for the initial release. The app layer should remain the primary authorization boundary because:
- the existing app is built around Prisma within a clean server-side service layer
- the current requirement is “authentication and strict ownership at the API layer”
- RLS can be added later, but it is not a substitute for per-request server-side auth checks

If RLS is introduced in the future, it should be treated as a second line of defense on top of the same user-scoped application logic and not as a replacement for validation or route authorization.

## 8. Proposal for the initial implementation sequence

The following order is recommended once the design is approved:

1. Install and configure the Supabase frontend client and the server-side Supabase verification dependency.
2. Add `api/.env` values for `SUPABASE_URL` and the least-privileged supported verification key (publishable/anon) or other required verification configuration. Do not require a service-role key solely for token verification.
3. Create frontend auth pages and protected route guards.
4. Add Express auth middleware and a trusted request context.
5. Add the internal user provisioning flow keyed by `supabaseAuthId`.
6. Add protected CRUD routes with same-user ownership checks.
7. Add tests for 401/403/404 flows and ownership mismatch cases.
8. Add password reset and email confirmation UX after the baseline auth flow is working.

This sequence preserves the current demo app behavior until the authenticated user flow is fully validated.

## 9. Acceptance criteria for the approved design

The design is considered approved when all of the following are true:

- Supabase Auth remains the system of record for credentials, session lifecycle, email confirmation, reset, and refresh.
- Express verifies the bearer token before any authorization logic runs.
- Internal identity resolution is based only on the verified Supabase UUID and `User.supabaseAuthId`.
- Every domain record is scoped by `userId` from the authenticated request context.
- No client-supplied identity data is ever trusted for ownership decisions.
- No additional schema or migration changes are introduced during this design phase; the existing auth-ID migration has already been applied.

## 10. Explicit implementation gate

Do not implement authentication or protected CRUD routes until this design is approved.

This document is intended to be the implementation contract for the next step. It does not propose additional Prisma schema changes or migrations; the existing `supabaseAuthId` migration is already applied.

FILE: generate-context.ps1
SIZE: 24683 bytes

#requires -Version 5.1
<#
.SYNOPSIS
    Generates an AI-readable snapshot of a software repository.

.DESCRIPTION
    - Detects the project root using $PSScriptRoot.
    - Creates project-context/project-context.txt.
    - Includes a directory tree, metadata, and relevant text files.
    - Excludes dependencies, generated output, environment files, and binaries.
    - Skips files suspected of containing hardcoded secrets.
    - Applies per-file and total-output size limits.
    - Does not execute project code, install packages, or modify source files.

.COMPATIBILITY
    Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7+.
#>

Set-StrictMode -Version 2.0
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'

# ----------------------------- Configuration -----------------------------

$OutputDirectoryName = 'project-context'
$OutputFileName = 'project-context.txt'

# Maximum size of an individual source file to include.
$MaxFileSizeBytes = 256KB

# Maximum approximate output size before a warning is displayed.
$MaxOutputSizeBytes = 5MB

# Set to $true to include hidden text files, excluding protected paths.
$IncludeHiddenFiles = $true

# Directory names that are never traversed.
$ExcludedDirectoryNames = @(
    '.git', '.svn', '.hg',
    'node_modules', 'bower_components',
    '.next', '.nuxt', '.output', '.turbo',
    'dist', 'build', 'out', 'release',
    'coverage', '.nyc_output',
    '__pycache__', '.pytest_cache', '.mypy_cache',
    '.ruff_cache', '.tox', '.venv', 'venv', 'env',
    'vendor', 'target', 'bin', 'obj',
    '.idea', '.vscode-test',
    'playwright-report', 'test-results',
    'storybook-static',
    $OutputDirectoryName
)

# Extensions commonly useful for software architecture and debugging.
$IncludedExtensions = @(
    '.ts', '.tsx', '.mts', '.cts',
    '.js', '.jsx', '.mjs', '.cjs',
    '.py', '.pyi',
    '.java', '.kt', '.kts', '.scala',
    '.c', '.h', '.cpp', '.hpp', '.cc',
    '.cs', '.fs', '.go', '.rs', '.php',
    '.rb', '.rake', '.swift', '.dart',
    '.vue', '.svelte', '.astro',
    '.html', '.htm', '.css', '.scss', '.sass', '.less',
    '.json', '.jsonc', '.json5',
    '.yaml', '.yml', '.toml', '.ini', '.cfg', '.conf',
    '.xml', '.xsd', '.xsl',
    '.sql', '.prisma', '.graphql', '.gql',
    '.md', '.mdx', '.rst', '.txt',
    '.ps1', '.psm1', '.psd1', '.sh', '.bash',
    '.bat', '.cmd',
    '.dockerfile', '.tf', '.hcl',
    '.properties', '.gradle',
    '.make', '.mk', '.cmake',
    '.proto', '.ipynb',
    '.editorconfig', '.gitignore', '.gitattributes',
    '.npmrc', '.nvmrc', '.prettierrc', '.eslintrc',
    '.lock'
)

# Important extensionless or specially named files.
$IncludedFileNames = @(
    'README', 'README.md', 'README.txt',
    'LICENSE', 'LICENSE.md', 'NOTICE',
    'CHANGELOG', 'CHANGELOG.md',
    'CONTRIBUTING.md', 'CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md',
    'SECURITY.md', 'SUPPORT.md',
    'Dockerfile', 'Containerfile',
    'Makefile', 'GNUmakefile',
    'Procfile', 'Justfile',
    'Jenkinsfile', 'Vagrantfile',
    'CMakeLists.txt',
    'package.json', 'package-lock.json',
    'npm-shrinkwrap.json', 'yarn.lock',
    'pnpm-lock.yaml', 'bun.lock', 'bun.lockb',
    'composer.json', 'composer.lock',
    'Cargo.toml', 'Cargo.lock',
    'go.mod', 'go.sum',
    'requirements.txt', 'Pipfile', 'Pipfile.lock',
    'poetry.lock', 'pyproject.toml',
    'uv.lock', 'PDM.lock',
    'Gemfile', 'Gemfile.lock',
    'mix.exs', 'mix.lock',
    'pubspec.yaml', 'pubspec.lock',
    'pom.xml', 'build.gradle', 'build.gradle.kts',
    'settings.gradle', 'settings.gradle.kts',
    'gradlew', 'mvnw',
    'global.json', 'NuGet.config',
    'Directory.Build.props', 'Directory.Build.targets',
    'tsconfig', 'jsconfig',
    '.gitignore', '.gitattributes', '.gitmodules',
    '.dockerignore', '.npmrc', '.nvmrc',
    '.editorconfig', '.prettierrc', '.eslintrc',
    '.env.example', '.env.sample', '.env.template',
    '.env.development.example', '.env.production.example',
    'LICENSE-MIT', 'LICENSE-APACHE'
)

# Never include real environment files, private keys, or certificate files.
$ExcludedFileNames = @(
    '.env', '.env.local', '.env.development', '.env.production',
    '.env.test', '.env.development.local', '.env.production.local',
    '.env.test.local', '.env.staging', '.env.staging.local',
    'id_rsa', 'id_dsa', 'id_ecdsa', 'id_ed25519',
    'known_hosts', 'authorized_keys',
    'credentials', 'credentials.json',
    'secrets.json', 'service-account.json',
    'serviceaccount.json'
)

$ExcludedExtensions = @(
    '.exe', '.dll', '.so', '.dylib', '.bin', '.dat',
    '.png', '.jpg', '.jpeg', '.gif', '.bmp', '.ico',
    '.webp', '.avif', '.tif', '.tiff',
    '.pdf', '.doc', '.docx', '.xls', '.xlsx',
    '.ppt', '.pptx', '.zip', '.7z', '.rar', '.tar',
    '.gz', '.bz2', '.xz', '.woff', '.woff2', '.ttf',
    '.otf', '.eot', '.mp3', '.mp4', '.mov', '.avi',
    '.wav', '.sqlite', '.db', '.class', '.jar', '.war',
    '.pyc', '.pyo', '.pyd', '.pem', '.key', '.p12',
    '.pfx', '.cer', '.crt', '.der'
)

# ----------------------------- Initialization -----------------------------

if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($PSScriptRoot)) {
    throw 'Cannot determine the script directory. Save this script as a .ps1 file and run it from PowerShell.'
}

$ProjectRoot = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($PSScriptRoot).TrimEnd(
    [System.IO.Path]::DirectorySeparatorChar,
    [System.IO.Path]::AltDirectorySeparatorChar
)

$OutputDirectory = Join-Path $ProjectRoot $OutputDirectoryName
$OutputPath = Join-Path $OutputDirectory $OutputFileName

$IncludedFiles = New-Object 'System.Collections.Generic.List[object]'
$SkippedFiles = New-Object 'System.Collections.Generic.List[object]'
$ManualReviewFiles = New-Object 'System.Collections.Generic.List[string]'
$Errors = New-Object 'System.Collections.Generic.List[string]'

$Utf8Strict = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false, $true)
$Utf8Output = New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($true)

$GeneratedAt = Get-Date
$OutputBuffer = New-Object System.Text.StringBuilder

# ----------------------------- Helper functions -----------------------------

function Write-ProgressMessage {
    param([string]$Message)
    Write-Host ('[Context] ' + $Message) -ForegroundColor Cyan
}

function Get-RelativePathCompat {
    param(
        [string]$BasePath,
        [string]$FullPath
    )

    # Avoid Path.GetRelativePath because it is unavailable on older .NET.
    $Base = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($BasePath).TrimEnd(
        [System.IO.Path]::DirectorySeparatorChar,
        [System.IO.Path]::AltDirectorySeparatorChar
    ) + [System.IO.Path]::DirectorySeparatorChar

    $Full = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($FullPath)

    if ($Full.StartsWith($Base, [System.StringComparison]::OrdinalIgnoreCase)) {
        return $Full.Substring($Base.Length).Replace('\', '/')
    }

    if ($Full.Equals($Base.TrimEnd('\'), [System.StringComparison]::OrdinalIgnoreCase)) {
        return '.'
    }

    return $Full.Replace('\', '/')
}

function Test-IsUnderPath {
    param(
        [string]$Path,
        [string]$ParentPath
    )

    $Parent = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($ParentPath).TrimEnd(
        [System.IO.Path]::DirectorySeparatorChar,
        [System.IO.Path]::AltDirectorySeparatorChar
    ) + [System.IO.Path]::DirectorySeparatorChar

    $Full = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($Path)

    return $Full.StartsWith(
        $Parent,
        [System.StringComparison]::OrdinalIgnoreCase
    )
}

function Test-IsEnvironmentFile {
    param([string]$Name)

    # Allow only explicitly named example/sample/template environment files.
    if ($Name -match '^\.env(\..+)?$') {
        return ($Name -notmatch '\.(example|sample|template)$')
    }

    return $false
}

function Test-IsExcludedPath {
    param([System.IO.FileInfo]$File)

    $Relative = Get-RelativePathCompat $ProjectRoot $File.FullName
    $Parts = $Relative -split '/'

    foreach ($Part in $Parts) {
        if ($Part -eq $OutputDirectoryName) {
            return $true
        }
    }

    if ($ExcludedFileNames -contains $File.Name) {
        return $true
    }

    if (Test-IsEnvironmentFile $File.Name) {
        return $true
    }

    if ($ExcludedExtensions -contains $File.Extension.ToLowerInvariant()) {
        return $true
    }

    # Private-key blocks are never suitable for an AI context snapshot.
    if ($File.Name -match '(?i)\.(pem|key|p12|pfx|keystore|jks)$') {
        return $true
    }

    # Common credential or secret file names.
    if ($File.Name -match '(?i)(secret|credential|private.?key|service.?account)' -and
        $File.Name -notmatch '(?i)(example|sample|template|schema|test)') {
        return $true
    }

    return $false
}

function Test-IsLikelyBinary {
    param([byte[]]$Bytes)

    if ($null -eq $Bytes -or $Bytes.Length -eq 0) {
        return $false
    }

    $CheckLength = [Math]::Min($Bytes.Length, 8192)

    for ($i = 0; $i -lt $CheckLength; $i++) {
        if ($Bytes[$i] -eq 0) {
            return $true
        }
    }

    return $false
}

function Get-TextFromBytes {
    param([byte[]]$Bytes)

    if ($null -eq $Bytes -or $Bytes.Length -eq 0) {
        return ''
    }

    # Recognize common Unicode byte-order marks first.
    if ($Bytes.Length -ge 3 -and
        $Bytes[0] -eq 0xEF -and $Bytes[1] -eq 0xBB -and $Bytes[2] -eq 0xBF) {
        return [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetString($Bytes, 3, $Bytes.Length - 3)
    }

    if ($Bytes.Length -ge 2 -and $Bytes[0] -eq 0xFF -and $Bytes[1] -eq 0xFE) {
        return [System.Text.Encoding]::Unicode.GetString($Bytes, 2, $Bytes.Length - 2)
    }

    if ($Bytes.Length -ge 2 -and $Bytes[0] -eq 0xFE -and $Bytes[1] -eq 0xFF) {
        return [System.Text.Encoding]::BigEndianUnicode.GetString($Bytes, 2, $Bytes.Length - 2)
    }

    try {
        return $Utf8Strict.GetString($Bytes)
    }
    catch {
        # Fallback for older Windows files saved using the active code page.
        return [System.Text.Encoding]::Default.GetString($Bytes)
    }
}

function Test-LikelySecretContent {
    param([string]$Content)

    # Heuristic patterns only. This is not a complete secret scanner.
    $Patterns = @(
        '(?im)-----BEGIN (?:RSA |EC |OPENSSH |DSA )?PRIVATE KEY-----',
        '(?im)\bAKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}\b',
        '(?im)\bASIA[0-9A-Z]{16}\b',
        '(?im)\bgh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9_]{20,}\b',
        '(?im)\bgithub_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{20,}\b',
        '(?im)\bsk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}\b',
        '(?im)\bAIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{30,}\b',
        '(?im)\bxox[baprs]-[A-Za-z0-9-]{10,}\b',
        '(?im)\beyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}\b',
        '(?im)\b(password|passwd|secret|api[_-]?key|access[_-]?token|auth[_-]?token|client[_-]?secret|private[_-]?key)\b\s*[:=]\s*["''][^"'']{8,}["'']',
        '(?im)\bBearer\s+[A-Za-z0-9._~+/=-]{20,}',
        '(?im)\bpostgres(?:ql)?://[^/\s:]+:[^@\s]+@[^/\s]+',
        '(?im)\bmongodb(?:\+srv)?://[^/\s:]+:[^@\s]+@[^/\s]+'
    )

    foreach ($Pattern in $Patterns) {
        if ($Content -match $Pattern) {
            return $true
        }
    }

    return $false
}

function Add-OutputLine {
    param([string]$Line)

    [void]$OutputBuffer.AppendLine($Line)
}

function Add-Section {
    param([string]$Title)

    Add-OutputLine ''
    Add-OutputLine ('=' * 88)
    Add-OutputLine $Title
    Add-OutputLine ('=' * 88)
}

function Get-TreeLines {
    param([string]$Root)

    $Lines = New-Object 'System.Collections.Generic.List[string]'
    $Queue = New-Object 'System.Collections.Generic.Queue[object]'
    $Queue.Enqueue(@{ Path = $Root; Prefix = '' })

    while ($Queue.Count -gt 0) {
        $Item = $Queue.Dequeue()
        $CurrentPath = [string]$Item.Path
        $Prefix = [string]$Item.Prefix

        try {
            $Children = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $CurrentPath -Force -ErrorAction Stop |
                Where-Object {
                    $ExcludedDirectoryNames -notcontains $_.Name -and
                    -not ($_.PSIsContainer -and $_.Name -eq $OutputDirectoryName) -and
                    -not (Test-IsEnvironmentFile $_.Name) -and
                    ($IncludeHiddenFiles -or $_.Name -notlike '.*')
                } |
                Sort-Object @{ Expression = { if ($_.PSIsContainer) { 0 } else { 1 } } }, Name)
        }
        catch {
            [void]$Lines.Add($Prefix + '[Unable to read directory]')
            continue
        }

        foreach ($Child in $Children) {
            if ($Child.PSIsContainer) {
                [void]$Lines.Add($Prefix + '+-- ' + $Child.Name + '/')
                $Queue.Enqueue(@{
                    Path = $Child.FullName
                    Prefix = $Prefix + '    '
                })
            }
            else {
                $LengthLabel = ''
                try {
                    $LengthLabel = ' (' + $Child.Length + ' bytes)'
                }
                catch {}

                [void]$Lines.Add($Prefix + '+-- ' + $Child.Name + $LengthLabel)
            }
        }
    }

    return $Lines
}

# ----------------------------- File collection -----------------------------

try {
    Write-ProgressMessage ('Project root: ' + $ProjectRoot)
    Write-ProgressMessage 'Preparing output directory...'

    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $OutputDirectory -PathType Container)) {
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Path $OutputDirectory -Force | Out-Null
    }

    # Resolve output paths before enumeration to avoid accidental self-inclusion.
    $OutputFullPath = [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($OutputPath)

    Write-ProgressMessage 'Scanning repository files...'

    $AllFiles = New-Object 'System.Collections.Generic.List[System.IO.FileInfo]'
    $DirectoryQueue = New-Object 'System.Collections.Generic.Queue[string]'
    $DirectoryQueue.Enqueue($ProjectRoot)

    while ($DirectoryQueue.Count -gt 0) {
        $CurrentDirectory = $DirectoryQueue.Dequeue()

        try {
            $Children = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $CurrentDirectory -Force -ErrorAction Stop |
                Sort-Object Name)

            foreach ($Child in $Children) {
                if ($Child.PSIsContainer) {
                    if ($ExcludedDirectoryNames -contains $Child.Name) {
                        continue
                    }

                    if (-not $IncludeHiddenFiles -and $Child.Name.StartsWith('.')) {
                        continue
                    }

                    if (Test-IsUnderPath $Child.FullName $OutputDirectory) {
                        continue
                    }

                    $DirectoryQueue.Enqueue($Child.FullName)
                }
                else {
                    if ($Child.FullName -eq $OutputFullPath) {
                        continue
                    }

                    if (-not $IncludeHiddenFiles -and $Child.Name.StartsWith('.')) {
                        continue
                    }

                    if (Test-IsExcludedPath $Child) {
                        $SkippedFiles.Add([PSCustomObject]@{
                            Path = Get-RelativePathCompat $ProjectRoot $Child.FullName
                            Reason = 'Excluded by safety or file-type rules'
                        }) | Out-Null
                        continue
                    }

                    $IsNamed = $IncludedFileNames -contains $Child.Name
                    $IsExtensionIncluded = $IncludedExtensions -contains $Child.Extension.ToLowerInvariant()

                    if ($IsNamed -or $IsExtensionIncluded) {
                        $AllFiles.Add($Child)
                    }
                    else {
                        $SkippedFiles.Add([PSCustomObject]@{
                            Path = Get-RelativePathCompat $ProjectRoot $Child.FullName
                            Reason = 'File type not selected'
                        }) | Out-Null
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        catch {
            $RelativeDirectory = Get-RelativePathCompat $ProjectRoot $CurrentDirectory
            $Message = 'Cannot enumerate directory "' + $RelativeDirectory + '": ' + $_.Exception.Message
            $Errors.Add($Message) | Out-Null
            Write-Warning $Message
        }
    }

    # Stable ordering makes repeated runs predictable.
    $AllFiles = @($AllFiles | Sort-Object {
        Get-RelativePathCompat $ProjectRoot $_.FullName
    })

    Write-ProgressMessage ('Found ' + $AllFiles.Count + ' candidate text files.')

    # ----------------------------- Build snapshot -----------------------------

    Add-OutputLine 'PROJECT CONTEXT SNAPSHOT'
    Add-OutputLine ('Generated: ' + $GeneratedAt.ToString('yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss zzz'))
    Add-OutputLine ('Project root: ' + $ProjectRoot)
    Add-OutputLine ('Output file: ' + $OutputDirectoryName + '/' + $OutputFileName)
    Add-OutputLine ('PowerShell version: ' + $PSVersionTable.PSVersion.ToString())
    Add-OutputLine ('Operating system: ' + [System.Environment]::OSVersion.VersionString)
    Add-OutputLine ''
    Add-OutputLine 'SECURITY NOTICE'
    Add-OutputLine 'Real .env files, common private-key files, binary files, and files matching'
    Add-OutputLine 'secret heuristics are excluded. Detection is heuristic and cannot guarantee'
    Add-OutputLine 'that every secret is found. Review the generated snapshot before uploading.'
    Add-OutputLine ''
    Add-OutputLine 'REPOSITORY OVERVIEW'
    Add-OutputLine ('Candidate files: ' + $AllFiles.Count)
    Add-OutputLine ('Excluded/skipped files so far: ' + $SkippedFiles.Count)

    Add-Section 'PROJECT DIRECTORY TREE'

    $TreeLines = @(Get-TreeLines -Root $ProjectRoot)
    foreach ($TreeLine in $TreeLines) {
        Add-OutputLine $TreeLine
    }

    Add-Section 'FILE CONTENTS'

    $IncludedCount = 0
    $IncludedBytes = 0
    $TotalSkippedForSize = 0

    foreach ($File in $AllFiles) {
        $RelativePath = Get-RelativePathCompat $ProjectRoot $File.FullName

        Write-ProgressMessage ('Inspecting: ' + $RelativePath)

        try {
            $CurrentFile = Get-Item -LiteralPath $File.FullName -ErrorAction Stop

            if ($CurrentFile.Length -gt $MaxFileSizeBytes) {
                $SkippedFiles.Add([PSCustomObject]@{
                    Path = $RelativePath
                    Reason = 'File exceeds per-file limit of ' + $MaxFileSizeBytes + ' bytes'
                }) | Out-Null
                $TotalSkippedForSize++
                continue
            }

            # Avoid reading the generated snapshot or any other file under output.
            if (([System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($CurrentFile.FullName)) -eq $OutputFullPath) {
                continue
            }

            $Bytes = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes($CurrentFile.FullName)

            if (Test-IsLikelyBinary -Bytes $Bytes) {
                $SkippedFiles.Add([PSCustomObject]@{
                    Path = $RelativePath
                    Reason = 'Binary content detected'
                }) | Out-Null
                continue
            }

            $Content = Get-TextFromBytes -Bytes $Bytes

            if (Test-LikelySecretContent -Content $Content) {
                $ManualReviewFiles.Add($RelativePath) | Out-Null
                $SkippedFiles.Add([PSCustomObject]@{
                    Path = $RelativePath
                    Reason = 'Possible hardcoded secret; excluded for manual review'
                }) | Out-Null
                continue
            }

            # Enforce the overall output budget before adding each file.
            $FileHeader = @(
                ''
                ('-' * 88)
                ('FILE: ' + $RelativePath)
                ('SIZE: ' + $CurrentFile.Length + ' bytes')
                ('-' * 88)
            ) -join [Environment]::NewLine

            $EstimatedAddition = [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetByteCount(
                $FileHeader + [Environment]::NewLine + $Content + [Environment]::NewLine
            )

            $CurrentOutputBytes = [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetByteCount(
                $OutputBuffer.ToString()
            )

            if (($CurrentOutputBytes + $EstimatedAddition) -gt $MaxOutputSizeBytes) {
                $SkippedFiles.Add([PSCustomObject]@{
                    Path = $RelativePath
                    Reason = 'Total output size limit reached'
                }) | Out-Null
                $TotalSkippedForSize++
                continue
            }

            [void]$OutputBuffer.AppendLine($FileHeader)
            [void]$OutputBuffer.AppendLine($Content)
            [void]$OutputBuffer.AppendLine('')

            $IncludedCount++
            $IncludedBytes += $CurrentFile.Length
        }
        catch {
            $ErrorMessage = 'Could not process "' + $RelativePath + '": ' + $_.Exception.Message
            $Errors.Add($ErrorMessage) | Out-Null
            $SkippedFiles.Add([PSCustomObject]@{
                Path = $RelativePath
                Reason = 'Read/processing error'
            }) | Out-Null
            Write-Warning $ErrorMessage
        }
    }

    # ----------------------------- Final report -----------------------------

    Add-Section 'GENERATION REPORT'
    Add-OutputLine ('Generation time: ' + $GeneratedAt.ToString('yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss zzz'))
    Add-OutputLine ('Project root: ' + $ProjectRoot)
    Add-OutputLine ('Included file count: ' + $IncludedCount)
    Add-OutputLine ('Skipped file count: ' + $SkippedFiles.Count)
    Add-OutputLine ('Included source bytes: ' + $IncludedBytes)
    Add-OutputLine ('Per-file size limit: ' + $MaxFileSizeBytes + ' bytes')
    Add-OutputLine ('Configured total-output limit: ' + $MaxOutputSizeBytes + ' bytes')
    Add-OutputLine ('Files skipped because of size limits: ' + $TotalSkippedForSize)
    Add-OutputLine ('Files requiring manual secret review: ' + $ManualReviewFiles.Count)
    Add-OutputLine ('Processing errors: ' + $Errors.Count)

    if ($ManualReviewFiles.Count -gt 0) {
        Add-OutputLine ''
        Add-OutputLine 'FILES EXCLUDED FOR POSSIBLE SECRETS - REVIEW MANUALLY'
        foreach ($ReviewPath in ($ManualReviewFiles | Sort-Object -Unique)) {
            Add-OutputLine (' - ' + $ReviewPath)
        }
        Add-OutputLine 'These files were not copied into the snapshot.'
    }

    if ($SkippedFiles.Count -gt 0) {
        Add-OutputLine ''
        Add-OutputLine 'SKIPPED FILES'
        foreach ($Skipped in ($SkippedFiles | Sort-Object Path)) {
            Add-OutputLine (' - ' + $Skipped.Path + ' :: ' + $Skipped.Reason)
        }
    }

    if ($Errors.Count -gt 0) {
        Add-OutputLine ''
        Add-OutputLine 'PROCESSING ERRORS'
        foreach ($ErrorEntry in $Errors) {
            Add-OutputLine (' - ' + $ErrorEntry)
        }
    }

    # Write once, replacing the previous snapshot instead of appending.
    $FinalText = $OutputBuffer.ToString()
    [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText($OutputFullPath, $FinalText, $Utf8Output)

    $FinalSize = (Get-Item -LiteralPath $OutputFullPath).Length

    Write-Host ''
    Write-Host 'Context generation complete.' -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host ('Output: ' + $OutputFullPath)
    Write-Host ('Included files: ' + $IncludedCount)
    Write-Host ('Skipped files: ' + $SkippedFiles.Count)
    Write-Host ('Files flagged for manual review: ' + $ManualReviewFiles.Count)
    Write-Host ('Final output size: ' + [Math]::Round($FinalSize / 1KB, 2) + ' KB')

    if ($FinalSize -gt $MaxOutputSizeBytes) {
        Write-Warning ('Final snapshot exceeds the configured total-output limit of ' +
            $MaxOutputSizeBytes + ' bytes. Consider lowering the limit or splitting the context.')
    }

    if ($ManualReviewFiles.Count -gt 0) {
        Write-Warning 'Some files were excluded because they may contain secrets. Review the report in the snapshot.'
    }

    Write-Host ''
    Write-Host 'Important: manually review the snapshot before uploading it to an AI service.' -ForegroundColor Yellow
}
catch {
    Write-Error ('Context generation failed: ' + $_.Exception.Message)
    exit 1
}

# CareerOS

CareerOS is a career-management workspace for applications, companies, contacts, tasks, and scheduled events. The current UI remains usable with browser-local demo data; PostgreSQL and Prisma are prepared as a backend foundation, but authentication and database-backed CRUD are not yet implemented.

## Features

- Responsive overview with data-derived metrics, a compact monthly calendar, upcoming tasks, and recent applications.
- Shared monthly calendar on the Overview and Calendar pages, with local-date-safe navigation, event/task indicators, selected-day details, and create/edit/delete actions.
- Editable Applications, Companies, Contacts, Tasks, and Events with browser-local persistence.
- Light and dark themes, persisted in this browser.
- Explicit demo mode and a user-triggered JSON export from Settings.
- Express health endpoint with database connection status; no unauthenticated record API.

## Stack

- React 19, strict TypeScript, Vite 7, Tailwind CSS 4, React Router 7
- TanStack Query, React Hook Form, Zod, date-fns, Lucide React
- Node.js, Express 5, Pino HTTP logging
- PostgreSQL, Prisma 7.10, `@prisma/adapter-pg`, and `pg`
- Vitest and Oxlint

## Project layout

```text
api/
  src/db/              Prisma singleton and read-only connection check
  src/                 Express app, configuration, and server lifecycle
  prisma.config.ts     Prisma 7 CLI configuration
  .env.example         Backend environment template
prisma/
  schema.prisma        PostgreSQL domain schema
  migrations/          Reviewed SQL migrations
src/
  components/          App shell, forms, and reusable monthly calendar
  context/             Demo data and persisted theme providers
  data/                Demo records and tests
  lib/                 Calendar date helpers
  pages/               Overview, directories, tasks, calendar, settings
```

## Local development

Requirements: Node.js 22+ and npm 10+.

```powershell
npm install
npm run dev
```

The frontend runs at `http://localhost:5173`; Express runs at `http://localhost:3001`. The frontend is proxied to the API for `/api` requests. Configure backend settings in `api/.env`; see [DATABASE_SETUP.md](./DATABASE_SETUP.md) for Supabase and Prisma instructions.

Useful checks:

```powershell
npm test
npm run lint
npm run build
npm run build:api
npm run prisma:validate
npm run prisma:generate
npm run db:check
```

## Data modes and preservation

The UI currently uses `DemoCareerDataProvider`. Editable demo records are saved to this browser's `localStorage` under `careeros-demo-v1`; they are not shared, authenticated, or synchronized. Existing browser data is retained. Settings can download a JSON backup; nothing is uploaded automatically. There is no import or cloud migration flow yet.

The sidebar labels this as a demo workspace. A configured database connection does not change the UI data provider or imply that the demo records are synchronized.

## Database architecture

Prisma models users, companies, contacts, job applications, tasks, events, and activity. Related records carry `userId`; composite foreign keys also enforce same-user company/contact/application ownership. User-owned records cascade only when the user is deleted. Deleting a referenced company, contact, or application is restricted until dependent links are removed or reassigned. Dates use PostgreSQL `timestamptz`; IDs use consistent CUIDs.

Events and tasks remain separate records. The shared calendar derives day indicators and selected-day details from both collections; completed tasks are omitted. The initial schema and migration are in `prisma/`.

## API and authentication boundary

`GET /api/health` reports API status and whether the database is connected, unavailable, configured, or not configured. The server's Prisma singleton is not exposed through record routes. There is no authentication, authorization, repository/service CRUD, or database-backed frontend provider. Do not add public data endpoints or accept client-supplied `userId` as proof of ownership. Secure authentication and user-scoped validation must be integrated before database CRUD or public deployment.

## Limitations

- Supabase credentials may be configured locally in the ignored `api/.env`, but this environment cannot currently resolve/connect to the supplied Supabase database host. No migration has been applied and database connectivity is not claimed.
- Demo data is not imported into PostgreSQL.
- No seed script, authentication, database CRUD, calendar sync, or external reminders are implemented.
- Public deployment requires authentication, authorization, rate limiting, production CORS/secrets, migration operations, and integration tests.

import { Suspense, lazy } from 'react'
import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from '@tanstack/react-query'
import { BrowserRouter, Route, Routes } from 'react-router-dom'
import { AppShell } from './components/AppShell'
import { DemoCareerDataProvider } from './context/CareerData'
import { ThemeProvider } from './context/ThemeProvider'

const Dashboard = lazy(() => import('./pages/Dashboard').then((module) => ({ default: module.Dashboard })))
const DirectoryPage = lazy(() => import('./pages/DirectoryPages').then((module) => ({ default: module.DirectoryPage })))
const TasksPage = lazy(() => import('./pages/DirectoryPages').then((module) => ({ default: module.TasksPage })))
const CalendarPage = lazy(() => import('./pages/DirectoryPages').then((module) => ({ default: module.CalendarPage })))
const SettingsPage = lazy(() => import('./pages/DirectoryPages').then((module) => ({ default: module.SettingsPage })))

const queryClient = new QueryClient({
  defaultOptions: { queries: { retry: 1, refetchOnWindowFocus: false } },
})

function NotFoundPage() {
  return <section className="directory-empty not-found"><span>404</span><h1>Page not found</h1><p>This page doesn’t exist in your CareerOS workspace.</p><a className="button button-secondary" href="/">Back to overview</a></section>
}

export default function App() {
  return <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
    <ThemeProvider>
      <DemoCareerDataProvider>
        <BrowserRouter>
          <Suspense fallback={<div className="route-loading">Opening your workspace…</div>}>
            <Routes>
              <Route element={<AppShell />}>
                <Route index element={<Dashboard />} />
                <Route path="applications" element={<DirectoryPage />} />
                <Route path="companies" element={<DirectoryPage />} />
                <Route path="contacts" element={<DirectoryPage />} />
                <Route path="tasks" element={<TasksPage />} />
                <Route path="calendar" element={<CalendarPage />} />
                <Route path="settings" element={<SettingsPage />} />
                <Route path="*" element={<NotFoundPage />} />
              </Route>
            </Routes>
          </Suspense>
        </BrowserRouter>
      </DemoCareerDataProvider>
    </ThemeProvider>
  </QueryClientProvider>
}

import { useEffect, useMemo, useRef, useState } from 'react'
import { NavLink, Outlet, useLocation, useNavigate } from 'react-router-dom'
import {
  Bell, BriefcaseBusiness, Building2, CalendarDays, CheckSquare, ChevronDown, CircleHelp,
  Command, LayoutDashboard, LogOut, Moon, Plus, Search, Settings, Sun, Users, X,
} from 'lucide-react'
import { getCompanyName } from '../data/demo'
import { useCareerData } from '../context/useCareerData'
import { useTheme } from '../context/useTheme'
import type { RecordType } from '../types'
import { RecordDialog } from './RecordDialog'

const navigation = [
  { label: 'Overview', to: '/', icon: LayoutDashboard },
  { label: 'Applications', to: '/applications', icon: BriefcaseBusiness },
  { label: 'Companies', to: '/companies', icon: Building2 },
  { label: 'Contacts', to: '/contacts', icon: Users },
  { label: 'Tasks', to: '/tasks', icon: CheckSquare },
  { label: 'Calendar', to: '/calendar', icon: CalendarDays },
]

const addOptions: { type: RecordType; label: string }[] = [
  { type: 'application', label: 'Application' },
  { type: 'company', label: 'Company' },
  { type: 'contact', label: 'Contact' },
  { type: 'task', label: 'Task' },
  { type: 'event', label: 'Event' },
]

const pathTitles: Record<string, string> = {
  '/': 'Overview',
  '/applications': 'Applications',
  '/companies': 'Companies',
  '/contacts': 'Contacts',
  '/tasks': 'Tasks & follow-ups',
  '/calendar': 'Calendar',
  '/settings': 'Settings',
}
export function AppShell() {
  const { data } = useCareerData()
  const { theme, toggleTheme } = useTheme()
  const location = useLocation()
  const navigate = useNavigate()
  const [search, setSearch] = useState('')
  const [quickAdd, setQuickAdd] = useState(false)
  const [addType, setAddType] = useState<RecordType | null>(null)
  const [menuOpen, setMenuOpen] = useState(false)
  const [profileOpen, setProfileOpen] = useState(false)
  const [noticeOpen, setNoticeOpen] = useState(false)
  const [sidebarOpen, setSidebarOpen] = useState(false)
  const [addInitialDate, setAddInitialDate] = useState<Date | undefined>()
  const searchInputRef = useRef<HTMLInputElement>(null)
  const title = pathTitles[location.pathname] ?? 'CareerOS'
  const query = search.trim().toLowerCase()

  useEffect(() => {
    const focusSearch = (event: KeyboardEvent) => {
      if ((event.metaKey || event.ctrlKey) && event.key.toLowerCase() === 'k') {
        event.preventDefault()
        searchInputRef.current?.focus()
      }
    }
    window.addEventListener('keydown', focusSearch)
    return () => window.removeEventListener('keydown', focusSearch)
  }, [])

  const results = useMemo(() => {
    if (query.length < 2) return []
    const matches = [
      ...data.companies.filter((item) => item.name.toLowerCase().includes(query)).map((item) => ({ label: item.name, detail: 'Company', path: '/companies' })),
      ...data.contacts.filter((item) => item.name.toLowerCase().includes(query) || getCompanyName(data, item.companyId).toLowerCase().includes(query)).map((item) => ({ label: item.name, detail: `Contact · ${getCompanyName(data, item.companyId)}`, path: '/contacts' })),
      ...data.applications.filter((item) => item.jobTitle.toLowerCase().includes(query) || getCompanyName(data, item.companyId).toLowerCase().includes(query)).map((item) => ({ label: item.jobTitle, detail: `Application · ${getCompanyName(data, item.companyId)}`, path: '/applications' })),
    ]
    return matches.slice(0, 6)
  }, [data, query])

  const goToSearch = (path: string) => {
    navigate(`${path}?q=${encodeURIComponent(search.trim())}`)
    setSearch('')
  }
  const openAdd = (type: RecordType, initialDate?: Date) => {
    setAddInitialDate(initialDate)
    setAddType(type)
  }

  return (
    <div className="app-shell">
      {sidebarOpen && <button className="mobile-scrim" aria-label="Close navigation" onClick={() => setSidebarOpen(false)} />}
      <aside className={`sidebar ${sidebarOpen ? 'sidebar-open' : ''}`}>
        <div className="brand-row">
          <div className="brand-mark">c</div>
          <span>careeros</span>
          <button className="mobile-close icon-button" aria-label="Close navigation" onClick={() => setSidebarOpen(false)}><X size={17} /></button>
        </div>
        <div className="workspace-switcher">
          <span className="workspace-avatar">A</span>
          <span className="workspace-copy"><strong>Personal workspace</strong><small>Free plan</small></span>
          <ChevronDown size={15} />
        </div>
        <div className="sidebar-section-label">WORKSPACE</div>
        <nav className="primary-nav" aria-label="Main navigation">
          {navigation.map(({ label, to, icon: Icon }) => (
            <NavLink key={to} to={to} end={to === '/'} onClick={() => setSidebarOpen(false)} className={({ isActive }) => `nav-link ${isActive ? 'active' : ''}`}>
              <Icon size={17} strokeWidth={1.8} /><span>{label}</span>
              {label === 'Tasks' && data.tasks.filter((task) => !task.done).length > 0 && <span className="nav-count">{data.tasks.filter((task) => !task.done).length}</span>}
            </NavLink>
          ))}
        </nav>
        <div className="sidebar-section-label management-label">MANAGE</div>
        <NavLink to="/settings" className={({ isActive }) => `nav-link ${isActive ? 'active' : ''}`}><Settings size={17} strokeWidth={1.8} /><span>Settings</span></NavLink>
        <div className="sidebar-bottom">
          <div className="demo-callout"><div className="demo-dot" /><span><strong>Demo workspace</strong><small>Data stays in this browser</small></span></div>
          <button className="user-card" onClick={() => setMenuOpen((open) => !open)}>
            <span className="user-avatar">AC</span>
            <span className="user-copy"><strong>Alex Carter</strong><small>Personal account</small></span>
            <ChevronDown size={15} />
          </button>
          {menuOpen && <div className="user-menu"><button onClick={() => { navigate('/settings'); setMenuOpen(false) }}><Settings size={15} /> Settings</button><button onClick={() => setMenuOpen(false)}><LogOut size={15} /> Close menu</button></div>}
        </div>
      </aside>

      <main className="main-panel">
        <header className="topbar">
          <div className="topbar-left">
            <button className="mobile-menu icon-button" aria-label="Open navigation" onClick={() => setSidebarOpen(true)}><Command size={18} /></button>
            <span className="breadcrumb-muted">Workspace</span><span className="breadcrumb-slash">/</span><span className="breadcrumb-current">{title}</span>
          </div>
          <div className="topbar-actions">
            <div className="global-search">
              <Search size={16} />
              <input ref={searchInputRef} value={search} onChange={(event) => setSearch(event.target.value)} onKeyDown={(event) => { if (event.key === 'Enter' && search.trim()) goToSearch(results[0]?.path ?? '/applications') }} placeholder="Search anything..." aria-label="Search companies, contacts, applications" />
              <kbd>⌘ K</kbd>
              {query.length >= 2 && <div className="search-results">
                {results.length ? results.map((result) => <button key={`${result.detail}-${result.label}`} onClick={() => goToSearch(result.path)}><Search size={14} /><span><strong>{result.label}</strong><small>{result.detail}</small></span></button>) : <p>No matches found</p>}
              </div>}
            </div>
            <div className="quick-add-wrap">
              <button className="button button-primary quick-add-button" onClick={() => setQuickAdd((open) => !open)}><Plus size={16} /> <span>Add new</span></button>
              {quickAdd && <div className="quick-add-menu">{addOptions.map((option) => <button key={option.type} onClick={() => { setAddType(option.type); setQuickAdd(false) }}><Plus size={14} /> {option.label}</button>)}</div>}
            </div>
            <div className="notice-wrap">
              <button className="icon-button theme-toggle" aria-label={`Switch to ${theme === 'light' ? 'dark' : 'light'} mode`} title={`${theme === 'light' ? 'Dark' : 'Light'} mode`} onClick={toggleTheme}>{theme === 'light' ? <Moon size={17} /> : <Sun size={17} />}</button>
            </div>
            <div className="notice-wrap">
              <button className={`icon-button notification-button ${noticeOpen ? 'notice-active' : ''}`} aria-label="Notifications" onClick={() => setNoticeOpen((open) => !open)}><Bell size={17} /><i /></button>
              {noticeOpen && <div className="notification-popover"><strong>You’re all caught up</strong><p>Upcoming interviews and follow-ups will appear here.</p><button onClick={() => { navigate('/calendar'); setNoticeOpen(false) }}>View your calendar</button></div>}
            </div>
            <div className="top-profile-wrap">
              <button className="top-avatar" aria-label="Open profile menu" aria-expanded={profileOpen} onClick={() => setProfileOpen((open) => !open)}>AC</button>
              {profileOpen && <div className="top-profile-menu"><strong>Alex Carter</strong><span>Personal demo account</span><button onClick={() => { navigate('/settings'); setProfileOpen(false) }}><Settings size={14} /> Settings</button></div>}
            </div>
          </div>
        </header>
        <div className="page-content"><Outlet context={{ openAdd }} /></div>
        <footer className="app-footer"><span>CareerOS <span className="footer-dot">·</span> Your next move, organized.</span><a href="mailto:hello@careeros.example"><CircleHelp size={14} /> Help</a></footer>
      </main>
      {addType && <RecordDialog type={addType} initialDate={addInitialDate} onClose={() => { setAddType(null); setAddInitialDate(undefined) }} />}
    </div>
  )
}

FILE: src/components/MonthlyCalendar.tsx

import { useMemo, useState } from 'react'
import { addMonths, format, isSameDay, isSameMonth, isToday, parseISO, startOfMonth, subMonths } from 'date-fns'
import { ArrowLeft, ArrowRight, CalendarPlus, CheckSquare, Clock3, MapPin, Plus, Trash2 } from 'lucide-react'
import { getCalendarDays, getItemsForDate, getScheduledItems } from '../lib/calendar'
import type { CareerData, RecordType } from '../types'

interface MonthlyCalendarProps {
  data: CareerData
  compact?: boolean
  onAdd: (type: Extract<RecordType, 'task' | 'event'>, date: Date) => void
  onOpen: (type: Extract<RecordType, 'task' | 'event'>, id: string) => void
  onDeleteEvent?: (id: string) => void
}

const weekdays = ['Mon', 'Tue', 'Wed', 'Thu', 'Fri', 'Sat', 'Sun']

export function MonthlyCalendar({ data, compact = false, onAdd, onOpen, onDeleteEvent }: MonthlyCalendarProps) {
  const [selectedDate, setSelectedDate] = useState(() => new Date())
  const [month, setMonth] = useState(() => startOfMonth(new Date()))
  const days = useMemo(() => getCalendarDays(month), [month])
  const items = useMemo(() => getScheduledItems(data), [data])
  const selectedItems = getItemsForDate(items, selectedDate)

  const goToMonth = (nextMonth: Date) => {
    setMonth(startOfMonth(nextMonth))
    setSelectedDate((selected) => isSameMonth(selected, nextMonth) ? selected : startOfMonth(nextMonth))
  }

  const selectToday = () => {
    const today = new Date()
    setMonth(startOfMonth(today))
    setSelectedDate(today)
  }

  return <section className={`monthly-calendar ${compact ? 'monthly-calendar-compact' : ''}`} aria-label="Monthly calendar">
    <div className="calendar-heading">
      <div><h2>{format(month, 'MMMM yyyy')}</h2><p>{selectedItems.length ? `${selectedItems.length} scheduled item${selectedItems.length === 1 ? '' : 's'} selected` : 'Select a date to see what’s planned'}</p></div>
      <div className="calendar-controls">
        <button className="calendar-today" onClick={selectToday}>Today</button>
        <button className="calendar-nav-button" aria-label="Previous month" onClick={() => goToMonth(subMonths(month, 1))}><ArrowLeft size={15} /></button>
        <button className="calendar-nav-button" aria-label="Next month" onClick={() => goToMonth(addMonths(month, 1))}><ArrowRight size={15} /></button>
      </div>
    </div>
    <div className="calendar-grid" role="grid" aria-label={format(month, 'MMMM yyyy')}>
      {weekdays.map((weekday) => <div className="calendar-weekday" role="columnheader" key={weekday}>{weekday}</div>)}
      {days.map((day) => {
        const dayItems = getItemsForDate(items, day)
        const hasEvent = dayItems.some((item) => item.type === 'event')
        const hasTask = dayItems.some((item) => item.type === 'task')
        const selected = isSameDay(day, selectedDate)
        return <button
          className={`calendar-day ${!isSameMonth(day, month) ? 'calendar-day-outside' : ''} ${isToday(day) ? 'calendar-day-today' : ''} ${selected ? 'calendar-day-selected' : ''}`}
          role="gridcell"
          aria-label={`${format(day, 'EEEE, MMMM d, yyyy')}${dayItems.length ? `, ${dayItems.length} scheduled item${dayItems.length === 1 ? '' : 's'}` : ''}`}
          aria-pressed={selected}
          key={format(day, 'yyyy-MM-dd')}
          onClick={() => setSelectedDate(day)}
        >
          <span className="calendar-day-number">{format(day, 'd')}</span>
          <span className="calendar-indicators" aria-hidden="true">
            {hasEvent && <i className="calendar-event-indicator" />}
            {hasTask && <i className="calendar-task-indicator" />}
          </span>
        </button>
      })}
    </div>
    <div className="calendar-selected">
      <div className="calendar-selected-heading">
        <h3>{isToday(selectedDate) ? 'Today' : format(selectedDate, 'EEEE, MMM d')}</h3>
        <div className="calendar-add-actions">
          <button className="calendar-add-link" onClick={() => onAdd('event', selectedDate)}><CalendarPlus size={13} /> Event</button>
          <button className="calendar-add-link" onClick={() => onAdd('task', selectedDate)}><Plus size={13} /> Task</button>
        </div>
      </div>
      {selectedItems.length ? <div className="calendar-selected-list">
        {selectedItems.map((item) => <article className={`calendar-selected-item calendar-selected-${item.type}`} key={`${item.type}-${item.id}`}>
          <span className="calendar-item-symbol" aria-hidden="true">{item.type === 'event' ? <Clock3 size={13} /> : <CheckSquare size={13} />}</span>
          <button className="calendar-item-open" onClick={() => onOpen(item.type, item.id)}>
            <strong>{item.title}</strong>
            <span>{item.type === 'event' ? `${item.category}${item.time ? ` · ${format(parseISO(item.time), 'h:mm a')}` : ''}` : `${item.category}${item.company ? ` · ${item.company}` : ''}`}</span>
            {item.type === 'event' && (item.location || item.company) && <small><MapPin size={11} /> {item.location || item.company}</small>}
          </button>
          {item.type === 'event' && onDeleteEvent && <button className="calendar-item-delete" aria-label={`Delete ${item.title}`} onClick={() => onDeleteEvent(item.id)}><Trash2 size={13} /></button>}
        </article>)}
      </div> : <div className="calendar-empty">Nothing scheduled. Add an event or task for this date.</div>}
      <p className="calendar-legend"><span><i className="calendar-event-indicator" /> Events</span><span><i className="calendar-task-indicator" /> Tasks</span></p>
    </div>
  </section>
}
